These match the TREC coarse-grained question classification categories exactly.

In TREC:
- ENTY (entity) covers things like animals, products, events, colors, creations, currencies, diseases, foods, instruments, languages, letters, organs, plants, religions, sports, substances, symbols, techniques, terms, vehicles, words
- DESC (description and abstract concept) covers definitions, descriptions, reasons, manners
- HUM (human being) covers individuals and groups of people
- LOC (location) covers cities, countries, mountains, states, other locations
- NUM (numeric value) covers counts, dates, distances, money, orders, percentages, periods, speeds, temperatures, sizes, weights
- ABBR (abbreviation) covers abbreviations and expressions abbreviated

Classification rules:
- 'abbreviation': Questions about what abbreviations/acronyms stand for, or questions about abbreviations themselves
- 'numeric value': Questions asking "how many", "how much", "what year", "what is the population", "what is the cost", or any question expecting a number/date as answer
- 'location': Questions asking about places, cities, countries, where something is located
- 'human being': Questions asking about a specific person (who wrote, who was, who invented, etc.)
- 'entity': Questions asking about a specific thing, organization, product, animal, etc. (what creature, what food, what character, name something)
- 'description and abstract concept': Questions asking for explanations, definitions, descriptions, reasons (what is X, why is, how can, what does X mean when it's not an abbreviation)
